ok give me an extremely detailed breakdown of surfmovements files and the changes we've made to utilise them in movmentsolver and actor

Here's a detailed breakdown:

1. **Surf State in Actor Class**
```cpp
// In actor.hpp
class Actor : public PtrHolder
{
private:
    bool mIsSurfing;   // Tracks if actor is currently surfing
    float mSurfSpeed;  // Current surf speed (optional, for stats)

public:
    void setSurfing(bool surfing) { mIsSurfing = surfing; }
    bool isSurfing() const { return mIsSurfing; }
    float getSurfSpeed() const { return mSurfSpeed; }
};

// In actor.cpp constructor initialization list
Actor::Actor(...) 
    : /* other initializations */
    , mIsSurfing(false)
    , mSurfSpeed(0.0f)
{
    // ... rest of constructor
}
```

2. **SurfMovements System (surfmovements.hpp)**
```cpp
namespace MWPhysics
{
    namespace SurfMovements
    {
        // Constants declared as extern
        extern const float SURF_ANGLE_MIN;    // Minimum angle for surf detection (45°)
        extern const float SURF_ANGLE_MAX;    // Maximum angle for surf detection (75°)
        extern const float SURF_FRICTION;     // Friction while surfing
        extern const float SURF_GRAVITY;      // Modified gravity while surfing
        extern const float AIR_ACCELERATE;    // Air strafing acceleration
        extern const float MAX_AIR_SPEED;     // Maximum achievable speed
        extern const float SURF_STICK_FORCE;  // Force keeping actor on surf

        // Core surfing functions
        bool isSurfableSurface(const btVector3& normal);  // Checks if surface angle is surfable
        void handleSurfMovement(ActorFrameData& actorData, const btVector3& normal, float dt);  // Main surf physics
        void applySurfingEffects(const MWWorld::Ptr& ptr);  // Sound/visual effects
        btVector3 calculateSurfVelocity(...);  // Calculate new velocity while surfing
        void updateSurfingState(Actor* actor, const btVector3& normal);  // Update actor's surf state
        float getSurfingSpeed(const btVector3& velocity);  // Get current surf speed
        bool shouldPreserveMomentum(...);  // Check if momentum should be preserved
    }
}
```

3. **SurfMovements Implementation (surfmovements.cpp)**
```cpp
namespace MWPhysics
{
    namespace SurfMovements
    {
        // Constants defined here
        const float SURF_ANGLE_MIN = 0.785f;   // 45 degrees in radians
        const float SURF_ANGLE_MAX = 1.309f;   // 75 degrees in radians
        // ... other constants

        bool isSurfableSurface(const btVector3& normal)
        {
            // Check if surface angle is between min and max
            float angle = normal.angle(btVector3(0, 0, 1));
            return (angle > SURF_ANGLE_MIN && angle < SURF_ANGLE_MAX);
        }

        void handleSurfMovement(ActorFrameData& actorData, const btVector3& normal, float dt)
        {
            btVector3 velocity = Misc::Convert::toBullet(actorData.mMovement);
            
            if (isSurfableSurface(normal))
            {
                // 1. Project velocity onto surface
                btVector3 surfaceNormal = normal.normalized();
                btVector3 projectedVelocity = velocity - (velocity.dot(surfaceNormal) * surfaceNormal);
                
                // 2. Apply modified gravity along surface
                btVector3 surfaceGravity = surfaceNormal * -SURF_GRAVITY * dt;
                projectedVelocity += surfaceGravity;
                
                // 3. Apply surf friction
                velocity = projectedVelocity * (1.0f - SURF_FRICTION);

                // 4. Handle air strafing
                const float sideMove = actorData.mMovement.x();
                if (std::abs(sideMove) > 0.0f)
                {
                    btVector3 wishDir = btVector3(sideMove > 0 ? 1 : -1, 0, 0);
                    float currentSpeed = velocity.length();
                    float addSpeed = std::min(MAX_AIR_SPEED - currentSpeed, AIR_ACCELERATE * dt);
                    if (addSpeed > 0)
                        velocity += wishDir * addSpeed;
                }

                // 5. Apply stick force to keep on surface
                velocity += surfaceNormal * -SURF_STICK_FORCE * dt;

                // 6. Apply speed limit
                float speed = velocity.length();
                if (speed > MAX_AIR_SPEED)
                {
                    float scale = MAX_AIR_SPEED / speed;
                    velocity *= scale;
                }

                // 7. Update movement and disable certain states
                actorData.mMovement = Misc::Convert::toOsg(velocity);
                actorData.mDidJump = false;
                actorData.mFlying = false;
                actorData.mSwimming = false;
            }
        }

        // ... other function implementations
    }
}
```

4. **MovementSolver Integration**
```cpp
// In movementsolver.cpp, inside the move function:
if (tracer.mFraction < 1.0f && !actor.mFlying)  // If we hit something and aren't flying
{
    btVector3 normal = Misc::Convert::toBullet(tracer.mPlaneNormal);
    
    // Check for surfable surface and handle surf movement
    if (SurfMovements::isSurfableSurface(normal))
    {
        SurfMovements::handleSurfMovement(actor, normal, time);
        
        // Apply effects for player only
        if (actor.mActorRaw->getPtr() == MWBase::Environment::get().getWorld()->getPlayerPtr())
            SurfMovements::applySurfingEffects(actor.mActorRaw->getPtr());
        
        // Update actor's surfing state
        SurfMovements::updateSurfingState(actor.mActorRaw, normal);
    }
}
```

The flow works like this:
1. MovementSolver detects collision with a surface
2. Checks if surface is surfable using `isSurfableSurface`
3. If surfable:
   - Applies surf physics with `handleSurfMovement`
   - Updates actor's surf state
   - Applies effects (for player)
4. The surf physics:
   - Projects movement onto surface
   - Applies reduced gravity
   - Handles air strafing
   - Maintains momentum
   - Applies speed limits

To tune the surfing feel, adjust the constants in `surfmovements.cpp`. Common adjustments:
- Lower `SURF_FRICTION` for smoother sliding
- Increase `AIR_ACCELERATE` for better air control
- Adjust `SURF_ANGLE_MIN/MAX` to change what surfaces are surfable
- Modify `SURF_GRAVITY` to change downward force while surfing

Would you like me to explain any particular part in more detail?

To test surfing in TES3MP, here's a step-by-step process:

1. **Create Test Ramps**
Use console commands to create ramps:
```

```
Or use existing architecture. Good places to test:
- Vivec, Foreign Quarter ramps
- Imperial fort ramps
- Or create custom ramps using `coc` to teleport to an empty cell and place objects

2. **Console Commands for Testing**
```
tcl           - Toggle collision (use to position yourself)
tgm           - Toggle god mode (prevent damage while testing)
player->setspeed 400  - Increase movement speed (optional)
```

3. **Test Cases to Try**:
   - Run up a ramp at around 45-75 degree angle
   - Try air strafing with A/D keys while in air
   - Chain multiple ramps together
   - Test different approach angles

4. **Debug Commands** (you might want to add these):
```cpp
// Add to console commands:
player->getsurfspeed    - Print current surf speed
togglesurfdebug         - Show surf angles/vectors
```

5. **What to Look For**:
   - Do you start surfing when hitting angled surfaces?
   - Does momentum feel preserved?
   - Does air strafing work?
   - Are you maintaining speed on ramps?

Let's add console commands to adjust surf settings in real-time. First, we need to modify `surfmovements.hpp` to make the constants adjustable:

```cpp
namespace MWPhysics
{
    namespace SurfMovements
    {
        // Change from const to regular variables
        extern float SURF_ANGLE_MIN;
        extern float SURF_ANGLE_MAX;
        extern float SURF_FRICTION;
        extern float SURF_GRAVITY;
        extern float AIR_ACCELERATE;
        extern float MAX_AIR_SPEED;
        extern float SURF_STICK_FORCE;

        // Add setter functions
        void setSurfAngleMin(float value);
        void setSurfAngleMax(float value);
        void setSurfFriction(float value);
        void setSurfGravity(float value);
        void setAirAccelerate(float value);
        void setMaxAirSpeed(float value);
        void setSurfStickForce(float value);

        // Add getter functions
        float getSurfAngleMin();
        float getSurfAngleMax();
        float getSurfFriction();
        float getSurfGravity();
```

Now create a new file `surfcommands.hpp` in `apps/openmw/mwscript/`:

```cpp
#ifndef GAME_SCRIPT_SURFCOMMANDS_H
#define GAME_SCRIPT_SURFCOMMANDS_H

#include <components/compiler/extension.hpp>
#include "../mwphysics/surfmovements.hpp"
#include "../mwbase/environment.hpp"
#include "../mwbase/windowmanager.hpp"

namespace MWScript
{
    namespace Surf
    {
        template<void (*Setter)(float), float (*Getter)()>
        class OpGetSet : public Interpreter::Opcode0
        {
            public:
                void execute(Interpreter::Runtime& runtime) override
                {
                    bool hasParam = runtime.getParameters() > 0;
                    if (hasParam)
                    {
                        float value = runtime[0].mFloat;
                        runtime.pop();
                        Setter(value);
                        MWBase::Environment::get().getWindowManager()->messageBox("Value set to: " + std::to_string(value));
                    }
                    else
                    {
                        float currentValue = Getter();
                        MWBase::Environment::get().getWindowManager()->messageBox("Current value: " + std::to_string(currentValue));
                    }
                }
        };

        void registerExtensions()
        {
            Interpreter::Interpreter& interpreter = MWBase::Environment::get().getScriptManager()->getInterpreter();

            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfAngleMin,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfAngleMin, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfAngleMin>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfAngleMax,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfAngleMax, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfAngleMax>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfFriction,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfFriction, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfFriction>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfGravity,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfGravity, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfGravity>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeAirAccelerate,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setAirAccelerate, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getAirAccelerate>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeMaxAirSpeed,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setMaxAirSpeed, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getMaxAirSpeed>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfStickForce,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfStickForce, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfStickForce>);
        }
    }
}

#endif
```

Add these opcodes in `components/compiler/opcodes.hpp`:

it looks like the opcodes are all in hexadecimal in that file

Ah yes, you're right! In `components/compiler/opcodes.hpp`, the opcodes should be in hexadecimal format. Here's the correct addition:

```cpp
namespace Compiler
{
    namespace Misc
    {
        // ... existing opcodes ...

        // Surf movement opcodes
        const int opcodeSurfAngleMin    = 0x3700;
        const int opcodeSurfAngleMax    = 0x3701;
        const int opcodeSurfFriction    = 0x3702;
        const int opcodeSurfGravity     = 0x3703;
        const int opcodeAirAccelerate   = 0x3704;
        const int opcodeMaxAirSpeed     = 0x3705;
        const int opcodeSurfStickForce  = 0x3706;
    }
}
```

The `0x` prefix indicates hexadecimal values. This matches the convention used in the rest of the file.

```cpp TES3MP\components\compiler\extensions.cpp
#include "extensions.hpp"

#include <cassert>
#include <stdexcept>

#include "generator.hpp"
#include "literals.hpp"

#include "surfcommands.hpp"

namespace Compiler
{
    Extensions::Extensions() : mNextKeywordIndex (-1) {}

    int Extensions::searchKeyword (const std::string& keyword) const
    {
        auto iter = mKeywords.find (keyword);
        if (iter==mKeywords.end())
            return 0;

        return iter->second;
    }

    bool Extensions::isFunction (int keyword, ScriptReturn& returnType, ScriptArgs& argumentType,
        bool& explicitReference) const
    {
        auto iter = mFunctions.find (keyword);
        if (iter==mFunctions.end())
            return false;

        if (explicitReference && iter->second.mCodeExplicit==-1)
            explicitReference = false;

        returnType = iter->second.mReturn;
        argumentType = iter->second.mArguments;
        return true;
    }

    bool Extensions::isInstruction (int keyword, ScriptArgs& argumentType,
        bool& explicitReference) const
    {
        auto iter = mInstructions.find (keyword);
        if (iter==mInstructions.end())
            return false;

        if (explicitReference && iter->second.mCodeExplicit==-1)
            explicitReference = false;

        argumentType = iter->second.mArguments;
        return true;
    }

    void Extensions::registerFunction (const std::string& keyword, ScriptReturn returnType,
        const ScriptArgs& argumentType, int code, int codeExplicit)
    {
        Function function;

        if (argumentType.find ('/')==std::string::npos)
        {
            function.mSegment = 5;
            assert (code>=33554432 && code<=67108863);
            assert (codeExplicit==-1 || (codeExplicit>=33554432 && codeExplicit<=67108863));
        }
        else
        {
            function.mSegment = 3;
            assert (code>=0x20000 && code<=0x2ffff);
            assert (codeExplicit==-1 || (codeExplicit>=0x20000 && codeExplicit<=0x2ffff));
        }

        int keywordIndex = mNextKeywordIndex--;

        mKeywords.insert (std::make_pair (keyword, keywordIndex));

        function.mReturn = returnType;
        function.mArguments = argumentType;
        function.mCode = code;
        function.mCodeExplicit = codeExplicit;

        mFunctions.insert (std::make_pair (keywordIndex, function));
    }

    void Extensions::registerInstruction (const std::string& keyword,
        const ScriptArgs& argumentType, int code, int codeExplicit)
    {
        Instruction instruction;

        if (argumentType.find ('/')==std::string::npos)
        {
            instruction.mSegment = 5;
            assert (code>=33554432 && code<=67108863);
            assert (codeExplicit==-1 || (codeExplicit>=33554432 && codeExplicit<=67108863));
        }
        else
        {
            instruction.mSegment = 3;
            assert (code>=0x20000 && code<=0x2ffff);
            assert (codeExplicit==-1 || (codeExplicit>=0x20000 && codeExplicit<=0x2ffff));
        }

        int keywordIndex = mNextKeywordIndex--;

        mKeywords.insert (std::make_pair (keyword, keywordIndex));

        instruction.mArguments = argumentType;
        instruction.mCode = code;
        instruction.mCodeExplicit = codeExplicit;

        mInstructions.insert (std::make_pair (keywordIndex, instruction));
    }

    void Extensions::generateFunctionCode (int keyword, std::vector<Interpreter::Type_Code>& code,
        Literals& literals, const std::string& id, int optionalArguments) const
    {
        assert (optionalArguments>=0);

        auto iter = mFunctions.find (keyword);
        if (iter==mFunctions.end())
            throw std::logic_error ("unknown custom function keyword");

        if (optionalArguments && iter->second.mSegment!=3)
            throw std::logic_error ("functions with optional arguments must be placed into segment 3");

        if (!id.empty())
        {
            if (iter->second.mCodeExplicit==-1)
                throw std::logic_error ("explicit references not supported");

            int index = literals.addString (id);
            Generator::pushInt (code, literals, index);
        }

        switch (iter->second.mSegment)
        {
            case 3:

                if (optionalArguments>=256)
                    throw std::logic_error ("number of optional arguments is too large for segment 3");

                code.push_back (Generator::segment3 (
                    id.empty() ? iter->second.mCode : iter->second.mCodeExplicit,
                    optionalArguments));

                break;

            case 5:

                code.push_back (Generator::segment5 (
                    id.empty() ? iter->second.mCode : iter->second.mCodeExplicit));

                break;

            default:

                throw std::logic_error ("unsupported code segment");
        }
    }

    void Extensions::generateInstructionCode (int keyword,
        std::vector<Interpreter::Type_Code>& code, Literals& literals, const std::string& id,
        int optionalArguments) const
    {
        assert (optionalArguments>=0);

        auto iter = mInstructions.find (keyword);
        if (iter==mInstructions.end())
            throw std::logic_error ("unknown custom instruction keyword");

        if (optionalArguments && iter->second.mSegment!=3)
            throw std::logic_error ("instructions with optional arguments must be placed into segment 3");

        if (!id.empty())
        {
            if (iter->second.mCodeExplicit==-1)
                throw std::logic_error ("explicit references not supported");

            int index = literals.addString (id);
            Generator::pushInt (code, literals, index);
        }

        switch (iter->second.mSegment)
        {
            case 3:

                if (optionalArguments>=256)
                    throw std::logic_error ("number of optional arguments is too large for segment 3");

                code.push_back (Generator::segment3 (
                    id.empty() ? iter->second.mCode : iter->second.mCodeExplicit,
                    optionalArguments));

                break;

            case 5:

                code.push_back (Generator::segment5 (
                    id.empty() ? iter->second.mCode : iter->second.mCodeExplicit));

                break;

            default:

                throw std::logic_error ("unsupported code segment");
        }
    }

    void Extensions::listKeywords (std::vector<std::string>& keywords) const
    {
        for (const auto & mKeyword : mKeywords)
            keywords.push_back (mKeyword.first);
    }
}
```

Let's break down all the changes and components we've added for surfing mechanics:

1. **Main Components Added**:
```
TES3MP/apps/openmw/mwphysics/surfmovements.hpp
TES3MP/apps/openmw/mwphysics/surfmovements.cpp
TES3MP/apps/openmw/mwscript/surfextensions.hpp
TES3MP/apps/openmw/mwscript/surfextensions.cpp
```

2. **Changes to Actor Class**:
```cpp
// In actor.hpp
private:
    bool mIsSurfing;
    float mSurfSpeed;
public:
    void setSurfing(bool surfing) { mIsSurfing = surfing; }
    bool isSurfing() const { return mIsSurfing; }
```

3. **Changes to MovementSolver**:
```cpp
// In movementsolver.cpp
if (tracer.mFraction < 1.0f && !actor.mFlying)
{
    btVector3 normal = Misc::Convert::toBullet(tracer.mPlaneNormal);
    if (SurfMovements::isSurfableSurface(normal))
    {
        SurfMovements::handleSurfMovement(actor, normal, time);
        // ... effects and state updates
    }
}
```

4. **Added Opcodes** (in `components/compiler/opcodes.hpp`):
```cpp
namespace Compiler
{
    namespace Misc
    {
        const int opcodeSurfAngleMin    = 0x3700;
        const int opcodeSurfAngleMax    = 0x3701;
        // ... other surf opcodes
    }
}
```

5. **Flow of Operation**:

a) **Initialization**:
- When game starts, surf variables are initialized in `SurfMovements` namespace
- Actor's surfing state starts as false

b) **Movement Detection**:
- `MovementSolver::move` handles all actor movement
- When collision occurs, it checks for surfable surfaces
- If surface angle is correct, surf mechanics activate

c) **Surf Physics**:
- `handleSurfMovement` manages surf physics:
  - Projects velocity onto surface
  - Applies modified gravity
  - Handles air strafing
  - Controls speed

d) **Console Commands**:
- When you type a command like `setsurffriction`:
  1. Game finds matching opcode
  2. Executes corresponding handler
  3. Updates value in `SurfMovements` namespace
  4. Shows feedback message

6. **Your Latest Change**:
```cpp
// In extensions.cpp
Surf::installOpcodes(extensions);
```
This line registers the surf-related console commands with the game's scripting system.

7. **Testing In-Game**:
```
setsurffriction 0.02    // Lower = more slippery
setairaccelerate 150    // Higher = more air control
setsurfgravity 300      // Lower = less downward force
```

The system works by:
1. Detecting collisions with surfaces
2. Checking if surface angle is within surf range
3. Applying special physics when surfing
4. Allowing real-time adjustment via console

explain opcodes in detail and how the opcodes we added in opcodes.hpp correspond to the change we made in apps/openmw/mwscript/extensions.cpp

Let's break down how opcodes work in OpenMW/TES3MP and how our surf additions fit in:

1. **Opcodes Overview**:
- Opcodes are numeric identifiers for game commands/instructions
- They tell the game's script interpreter what action to perform
- In OpenMW, they're organized in different segments and ranges

2. **Our Added Opcodes** (in `components/compiler/opcodes.hpp`):
```cpp
namespace Compiler
{
    namespace Misc
    {
        const int opcodeSurfAngleMin    = 0x3700;  // 14080 in decimal
        const int opcodeSurfAngleMax    = 0x3701;
        const int opcodeSurfFriction    = 0x3702;
        const int opcodeSurfGravity     = 0x3703;
        const int opcodeAirAccelerate   = 0x3704;
        const int opcodeMaxAirSpeed     = 0x3705;
        const int opcodeSurfStickForce  = 0x3706;
    }
}
```

3. **Extension System** (in `apps/openmw/mwscript/extensions.cpp`):
```cpp
namespace MWScript
{
    void installOpcodes(Interpreter::Interpreter& interpreter, bool consoleOnly)
    {
        // ... other installations ...
        Surf::installOpcodes(extensions);  // Our addition
    }
}
```

4. **The Connection**:
- When we define an opcode in `opcodes.hpp`, we're reserving a unique number for our command
- The installation in `extensions.cpp` connects these numbers to actual functionality
- The flow goes:
  ```
  Console Command -> Opcode Number -> Function Execution
  ```

5. **Complete Chain**:
```
Console: setsurffriction 0.02
↓
Opcode: 0x3702 (from opcodes.hpp)
↓
Extension Registration (in surfextensions.cpp)
↓
Function Execution: setSurfFriction(0.02)
```

6. **Why This Range**:
- OpenMW uses different opcode ranges for different purposes:
  - 0x2000-0x2FFF: Base game functions
  - 0x3000-0x3FFF: Extension functions
  - We chose 0x3700 to avoid conflicts with existing opcodes

7. **Actual Implementation Chain**:
```cpp
// 1. Opcode Definition (opcodes.hpp)
const int opcodeSurfFriction = 0x3702;

// 2. Extension Installation (surfextensions.cpp)
void installOpcodes(Compiler::Extensions& extensions)
{
    extensions.registerInstruction("setsurffriction", "", 
        Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfFriction);
}

// 3. Opcode Registration (extensions.cpp)
Surf::installOpcodes(extensions);

// 4. Function Execution (surfmovements.cpp)
void setSurfFriction(float value) { SURF_FRICTION = value; }
```

8. **Why Extensions.cpp Change**:
- The line `Surf::installOpcodes(extensions);` is crucial because:
  - It tells the game about our new commands
  - Links the opcode numbers to actual functions
  - Makes commands available in the console

9. **Command Flow Example**:
```
You type: setsurffriction 0.02
↓
Game looks up opcode for "setsurffriction" (0x3702)
↓
Finds registered handler in extensions
↓
Executes setSurfFriction(0.02)
↓
Updates SURF_FRICTION value
```

The key is that `opcodes.hpp` defines the "what" (unique identifiers), and `extensions.cpp` defines the "how" (linking those identifiers to actual functionality). Our change in `extensions.cpp` bridges this gap for our surf commands.

Would you like me to explain any specific part of this system in more detail?

ok im going to recompile and test, how can i get a console window with log information to launch alongside tes3mp.exe? should i build debug release or relwithdebug

For debugging and log information, you'll want to:

1. Build the `Debug` configuration rather than `Release` - this will give you more detailed logging information

2. Launch TES3MP from command prompt to see log output:
```bash
cd path\to\TES3MP\build\bin\Debug
tes3mp.exe --console
```

The `--console` flag will keep the console window open with log information.

Alternatively, you can also create a batch file (e.g., `debug_launch.bat`) with:
```batch
@echo on
tes3mp.exe --console
pause
```

This will:
- Launch the game with console output
- Keep the window open even if the game crashes
- Show you any error messages or debug information

When testing the surf mechanics, watch the console for:
- Physics-related messages
- Any errors in the surf calculations
- Debug information about surface angles and collisions

Yes! We need to add our new files to `TES3MP/apps/openmw/CMakeLists.txt`. Find these sections and add our new files:

```cmake
add_openmw_dir (mwphysics
    physicssystem trace collisiontype actor convert object heightfield closestnotmerayresultcallback
    contacttestresultcallback deepestnotmecontacttestresultcallback stepper movementsolver projectile
    actorconvexcallback raycasting mtphysics contacttestwrapper projectileconvexcallback surfmovements
```

We need to modify `surfextensions.hpp` and `surfextensions.cpp` to match this change:

```cpp
// surfextensions.hpp
#ifndef GAME_MWSCRIPT_SURFEXTENSIONS_H
#define GAME_MWSCRIPT_SURFEXTENSIONS_H

#include <components/interpreter/interpreter.hpp>

namespace MWScript
{
    namespace Surf
    {
        void installOpcodes(Interpreter::Interpreter& interpreter);
    }
}

#endif
```

```cpp
// surfextensions.cpp
#include "surfextensions.hpp"

#include <components/compiler/opcodes.hpp>
#include "../mwbase/environment.hpp"
#include "../mwbase/windowmanager.hpp"
#include "../mwphysics/surfmovements.hpp"

namespace MWScript
{
    namespace Surf
    {
        template<void (*Setter)(float), float (*Getter)()>
        class OpGetSet : public Interpreter::Opcode0
        {
            public:
                void execute(Interpreter::Runtime& runtime) override
                {
                    bool hasParam = runtime.getParameters() > 0;
                    if (hasParam)
                    {
                        float value = runtime[0].mFloat;
                        runtime.pop();
                        Setter(value);
                        MWBase::Environment::get().getWindowManager()->messageBox("Value set to: " + std::to_string(value));
                    }
                    else
                    {
                        float currentValue = Getter();
                        MWBase::Environment::get().getWindowManager()->messageBox("Current value: " + std::to_string(currentValue));
                    }
                }
        };

        void installOpcodes(Interpreter::Interpreter& interpreter)
        {
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfAngleMin,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfAngleMin, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfAngleMin>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfAngleMax,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfAngleMax, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfAngleMax>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfFriction,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfFriction, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfFriction>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfGravity,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfGravity, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfGravity>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeAirAccelerate,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setAirAccelerate, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getAirAccelerate>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeMaxAirSpeed,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setMaxAirSpeed, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getMaxAirSpeed>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfStickForce,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfStickForce, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfStickForce>);
        }
    }
}
```

This changes the approach to directly install the opcodes to the interpreter rather than using the extensions system. The changes ensure we're using the correct approach for registering our new commands.

Here are the correct, cleaned-up versions:

```cpp
// surfcommands.hpp
#ifndef GAME_SCRIPT_SURFCOMMANDS_H
#define GAME_SCRIPT_SURFCOMMANDS_H

#include <components/compiler/interpreter.hpp>
#include "../mwbase/environment.hpp"
#include "../mwworld/ptr.hpp"
#include <components/compiler/extension.hpp>
#include <components/compiler/opcodes.hpp>

namespace MWScript
{
    namespace Surf
    {
        void registerExtensions();
    }
}

#endif
```

```cpp
// surfcommands.cpp
#include "surfcommands.hpp"

#include <components/compiler/opcodes.hpp>
#include "../mwbase/environment.hpp"
#include "../mwbase/windowmanager.hpp"
#include "../mwphysics/surfmovements.hpp"

namespace MWScript
{
    namespace Surf
    {
        template<void (*Setter)(float), float (*Getter)()>
        class OpGetSet : public Interpreter::Opcode0
        {
            public:
                void execute(Interpreter::Runtime& runtime) override
                {
                    bool hasParam = runtime.getParameters() > 0;
                    if (hasParam)
                    {
                        float value = runtime[0].mFloat;
                        runtime.pop();
                        Setter(value);
                        MWBase::Environment::get().getWindowManager()->messageBox("Value set to: " + std::to_string(value));
                    }
                    else
                    {
                        float currentValue = Getter();
                        MWBase::Environment::get().getWindowManager()->messageBox("Current value: " + std::to_string(currentValue));
                    }
                }
        };

        void registerExtensions()
        {
            Interpreter::Interpreter& interpreter = MWBase::Environment::get().getScriptManager()->getInterpreter();

            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfAngleMin,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfAngleMin, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfAngleMin>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfAngleMax,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfAngleMax, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfAngleMax>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfFriction,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfFriction, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfFriction>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfGravity,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfGravity, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfGravity>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeAirAccelerate,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setAirAccelerate, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getAirAccelerate>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeMaxAirSpeed,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setMaxAirSpeed, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getMaxAirSpeed>);
            interpreter.installSegment5(Compiler::Misc::opcodeSurfStickForce,
                new OpGetSet<&MWPhysics::SurfMovements::setSurfStickForce, &MWPhysics::SurfMovements::getSurfStickForce>);
        }
    }
}
```

Then in `extensions.cpp`, just make sure you have:
```cpp
#include "surfcommands.hpp"

namespace MWScript
{
    void installOpcodes (Interpreter::Interpreter& interpreter, bool consoleOnly)
    {
        // ... other opcodes ...
        
        Surf::registerExtensions();  // Add this line
        
        if (consoleOnly)
        // ... rest of the function
    }
}
```

This version:
1. Uses a single consistent approach for registering commands
2. Properly integrates with OpenMW's scripting system
3. Provides both getter and setter functionality for surf parameters
4. Shows feedback messages in-game

The commands will work the same way as before, but the implementation is cleaner and more consistent with OpenMW's design patterns.
